=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    //return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return 'counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word) + 1
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i - truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res

=== PROMPT ===
Observe the following faulty python code. It contains one or more bugs.
Fix the code and output only the fixed code.

```python
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    //return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return 'counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word) + 1
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i - truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

Reason in a repair-and-recheck way.

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

```
class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        forbidden_set = set(forbidden)
        left = 0
        ans = 0
        for right in range(len(word)):
            for k in range(right, left-1, -1):
                if word[k:right+1] in forbidden_set:
                    left = k + 1
                    break
            ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
        return ans
```

        if word[left] not in node:
            break
        node = node[word[left]]
        if 'end' in node:
            left = left + 1
            break
    ans = max(ans, right-left+1)
```

```
def forbidden_len(word, i, j):
    t = trie
    cnt = 0
    for k in range(i, j):
        c = word[k]
        if c not in t:
            return 0
        t = t[c]
        cnt += 1
        if "end" in t:
            return cnt
    return 0
```

    truc = forbidden_len(word, i, j)
    if truc:
        j = i + truc - 1
    res = max(res, j - i)
return res
```

Now, let's decide final code style. We can write:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def forbidden_len(left: int, right: int) -> int:
            t = trie
            cnt = 0
            for k in range(left, right):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return 0
                t = t[c]
                cnt += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return cnt
            return 0

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            length = forbidden_len(i, j)
            if length:
                j = i + length - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True
        
        def isForbidden(s):
            t = trie
            counter = 0
            for c in s:
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                counter += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return counter
            return False
        
        res = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            truc = isForbidden(word[i:j])
            if truc:
                j = i + truc - 1
            res = max(res, j - i)
        return res
```

This fixes syntax and logic but keeps slicing. It may be acceptable. But if we want efficient, use index version. Which one is "fixed code"? The prompt says "Observe faulty code. The task likely expects a correct solution; LeetCode constraints require efficient. We should output efficient version.

But if no forbidden, trie empty, isForbidden would return False after first char, but the slice `word[i:j]` is created before the call, copying O(n-i) characters. So O(n^2). Definitely TLE for n=1e5. So we should avoid slicing. The index version is better and still simple.

Let's write final code with index version:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(l: int, r: int):
            t = trie
            cnt = 0
            for k in range(l, r):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                cnt += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return cnt
            return False

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            cnt = isForbidden(i, j)
            if cnt:
                j = i + cnt - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

Need maybe if `isForbidden` returns `False` and `cnt` is bool; `if cnt:` okay. If returns 0? It never returns 0. Good.

Let's test with word empty: j=0, loop empty, ans=0. Good.

Algorithm invariant: At start of iteration i, `word[i+1:j]` is valid and `j` is the maximum possible right boundary for any valid substring starting at i+1? Actually after processing i+1, j was set to the max right boundary for i+1. Let's prove by induction.

Base: i = n-1. Before loop, word[n:j]? empty valid? j=n. For i=n-1, we check prefix at n-1. If forbidden prefix length cnt, set j=n-1+cnt-1. Then word[n-1:j] length cnt-1 valid. If no forbidden prefix, j=n, word[n-1:n] valid. Also any longer substring starting at n-1? Only length1, so j is max. Good.

Inductive step: Assume after processing i+1, j is the maximum exclusive right boundary such that word[i+1:j] is valid (and word[i+1:j] valid). For i, consider candidate word[i:j]. If no forbidden prefix at i within word[i:j], then word[i:j] valid. Could there be a valid substring word[i:k] with k>j? If k>j, then word[i+1:k] would be a valid? Wait word[i:k] valid implies every substring of it valid, including word[i+1:k]. But word[i+1:k] is longer than word[i+1:j] (since k>j), contradicting maximality of j for i+1. Therefore no k>j. So j is max for i. If there is a forbidden prefix at i of length cnt, then any valid substring starting at i must have length < cnt, so max right boundary <= i+cnt-1. Also word[i:i+cnt-1] is valid? It has length cnt-1 and does not include the full forbidden prefix; but could it contain another forbidden substring starting later? Since it is a prefix of word[i:j]? Actually word[i:i+cnt-1] is contained in word[i:j]? Since cnt <= j-i, i+cnt-1 <= j-1, so yes it's within word[i:j]. But word[i:j] may be invalid due to the forbidden prefix; removing the last char of the forbidden prefix yields word[i:i+cnt-1], which does not contain that forbidden prefix. Could it contain another forbidden substring starting at i+1 or later? That substring would also be in word[i+1:j]? Since i+cnt-1 <= j-1, word[i+1:i+cnt-1] is a prefix of word[i+1:j], which is valid, so no. Thus word[i:i+cnt-1] valid. So max right boundary is i+cnt-1. Good. Thus induction holds.

This proof assumes if no forbidden prefix at i, word[i:j] valid because word[i+1:j] valid and no forbidden starting at i. Any forbidden in word[i:j] either starts at i or at >=i+1; if starts at >=i+1, it's in word[i+1:j], invalid. Good.

Thus algorithm correct.

Now, let's consider if the final code should be exactly:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(l, r):
            t = trie
            cnt = 0
            for k in range(l, r):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                cnt += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return cnt
            return False

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)
        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            cnt = isForbidden(i, j)
            if cnt:
                j = i + cnt - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)
        return ans
```

This is good.

Let's final with code.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(l: int, r: int):
            t = trie
            cnt = 0
            for k in range(l, r):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                cnt += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return cnt
            return False

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)

        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            cnt = isForbidden(i, j)
            if cnt:
                j = i + cnt - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestValidSubstring(self, word: str, forbidden: List[str]) -> int:
        trie = {}
        for f in forbidden:
            t = trie
            for c in f:
                if c not in t:
                    t[c] = {}
                t = t[c]
            t["end"] = True

        def isForbidden(l: int, r: int):
            t = trie
            cnt = 0
            for k in range(l, r):
                c = word[k]
                if c not in t:
                    return False
                t = t[c]
                cnt += 1
                if "end" in t:
                    return cnt
            return False

        ans = 0
        j = len(word)

        for i in range(len(word) - 1, -1, -1):
            cnt = isForbidden(i, j)
            if cnt:
                j = i + cnt - 1
            ans = max(ans, j - i)

        return ans
